21	
22	## Finding
23	The Rust `rust-public-functions` recipe is fragile on ast-grep 0.42.2 and "Known ast-grep Limitations" has no Rust entry covering it.
24	
25	## Expected
26	`find_code_by_rule` with the documented `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET` / `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)` patterns should return public functions; the limitations section should warn when it won't.
27	
28	## Actual
29	On ast-grep 0.42.2 the any-pattern rule returned "No matches found" for a crate with 200+ public fns; bare `pub fn $NAME` (find_code) matched but over-captured `pub(crate) fn` (not public API). Only Python/TSX limitations are documented.
30	
31	## Evidence
32	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/extraction-patterns.md:296-305` — `rust-public-functions` recipe (`pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET` / `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)`)
33	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/extraction-patterns.md:382-401` — "Known ast-grep Limitations" lists items #1–7, none Rust
34	- Live: `find_code_by_rule` rust any-pattern → "No matches found"; `find_code "pub fn $NAME"` → 232 matches incl. `pub(crate) fn send_command`
35	
36	## Impact
37	Forced a degrade from rule-based extraction to targeted source-grep pinned against the AN-verified surface — extra steps; risk of silent under-extraction for a Rust skill that trusted the recipe blindly.
38	
39	## Suggested Fix
40	Add a Rust entry to "Known ast-grep Limitations": document the 0.42.x any-pattern zero-match and the `pub fn $NAME` → `pub(crate)` over-match, and recommend a visibility-constrained rule or source-grep fallback (mirroring the Python class-pattern guidance).

23	
24	## Finding
25	§9b tells the operator to record `ides: ["{ides_written}"]` via `skf-manifest-ops.py set`, but that helper command has no `ides` parameter and writes `ides: []` for any new version.
26	
27	## Expected
28	`python3 {manifestOpsHelper} {skills_output_folder} set {skill} {version}` should merge `ides_written` into the version entry's `ides` (per §9b: "Each set invocation merges the per-version `ides` and `last_exported` fields" / "union its existing `ides` with `ides_written`").
29	
30	## Actual
31	`cmd_set(manifest_path, skill_name, version)` takes no ides argument; for a new version it writes `"ides": existing_version.get("ides", [])` → `[]`. The §9b-required end state (`ides: ["claude-code"]`) is unreachable through the documented call.
32	
33	## Evidence
34	- `src/skf-export-skill/references/update-context.md:335` — "`python3 {manifestOpsHelper} {skills_output_folder} set {skill-name} {version}`"

36	- `src/skf-export-skill/references/update-context.md:332` — "union its existing `ides` with `ides_written` (deduplicate, keep sorted)"
37	- `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-manifest-ops.py:127` — `def cmd_set(manifest_path, skill_name, version)` (no ides param)
38	- `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-manifest-ops.py:146` — `"ides": existing_version.get("ides", existing_version.get("platforms", []))`
39	
40	## Impact
41	Required a manual post-`set` JSON patch this run to record `ides: ["claude-code"]`; without it every exported skill's manifest entry has `ides: []`, breaking orphan detection and future managed-section rebuilds that key off recorded IDEs.
42	
43	## Suggested Fix
44	Add a repeatable/comma-separated `--ides` option to `skf-manifest-ops.py set` that unions into the version entry's `ides` server-side, and update §9b to call `set {skill} {version} --ides {ides_written}`.

26	
27	## Expected
28	§2.4 should evaluate cross-use against the skill's foregrounded API over the union of all usage-family sections present, with a split-body allowance.

30	## Actual
31	§2.1 matched a single usage anchor (`Quick Start`); §2.4's single-span scan would flag every method documented in `Common Workflows`, `Key API Summary`, and the 4 `references/*.md` files but not repeated inside the Quick Start code block — as binding High severity.
32	
33	## Evidence

36	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/coherence-check.md:27` — "severity assignments are binding; do not relax them"
37	- Skill under test had `## Quick Start` AND `## Common Workflows` (both §2.1 usage-family synonyms) plus split-body reference files; coverage was a verified 67/67 / score 99.3% — yet literal §2.4 yields ~25 High findings.
38	
39	## Impact
40	Forced a documented deviation from a "binding" instruction during this run (recorded as 1 Info instead of ~25 High) to avoid a nonsensical FAIL verdict on a passing skill; without the deviation the workflow would mis-grade well-formed split-body skills.
41	
42	## Suggested Fix
43	Add a split-body carve-out to §2.4: when `references/` exists and `## Full` sections are stubs, a method documented in any reference file or any usage-family section (Quick Start ∪ Common Workflows ∪ Key API Summary) satisfies the check; reserve High only for a foregrounded-API export absent from every usage-family section AND every reference file.

# FINDING 1 (fp-21c9342, bug) — ACCURATE

**Evidence — every skill-check `--help` probe in validate.md:**

- Line 64 (§2, primary probe):
  ```bash
  npx skill-check --help 2>/dev/null | grep -- --no-security-scan
  ```
- Line 68 (§2, secondary probe, inline):
  > `- Else run a second probe — `npx skill-check --help 2>/dev/null | grep -- --skip-security` — and if it matches, set `{security_scan_flag} = "--skip-security"`.`

Line 69 is the consume of a probe miss: it sets `{skill_check_flag_fallback} = true`, skips §2/§4 automated flows, and falls to §3 manual-only validation.

**CLI surface confirmed (live probe, npx available, exit 0 both):**

- `npx skill-check --help` (top level) lists ONLY: `-h, --help` and the commands. **No `--no-security-scan`, no `--skip-security`, no flags at all.**
- `npx skill-check check --help` (the `check` subcommand) DOES list `--no-security-scan` (and `--security-scan`). Note: `--skip-security` does **not** exist anywhere — the real flag is `--no-security-scan`; the secondary `--skip-security` probe is a fallback for an alternate spelling that this installed version does not use.

So both line 64 and line 68 probes grep the top-level help, which never contains either flag. The probe always misses → line 69 always fires → `skill_check_flag_fallback=true` → §2/§4 automated validation is permanently skipped, degrading every run to §3 manual-only frontmatter validation. The bug is real and reproduced against the installed CLI (version-confirmed: `--no-security-scan` is defined on the `check` subcommand only).

**Precise minimal fix — exact before/after:**

Line 64, replace:
```bash
npx skill-check --help 2>/dev/null | grep -- --no-security-scan
```
with:
```bash
npx skill-check check --help 2>/dev/null | grep -- --no-security-scan
```

Line 68, replace the inline command:
```
`npx skill-check --help 2>/dev/null | grep -- --skip-security`
```
with:
```
`npx skill-check check --help 2>/dev/null | grep -- --skip-security`
```
(insert `check ` after `skill-check `; the rest of the sentence is unchanged).

**Other grep-of-help locations:** None. A repo-wide search found `skill-check -h` availability checks in `skf-quick-skill/.../write-and-validate.md:69`, `skf-create-stack-skill/.../validate.md:46`, `skf-test-skill/.../external-validators.md:50`, `skf-create-skill/.../validate.md:50`, `skf-update-skill/.../validate.md:23` — all are tool-presence checks (run `-h`, check exit), none pipe `--help` into `grep` for a flag, so none share this bug. Only validate.md:64 and :68 need the fix.

**Regression / breaking-change risk:** Minimal and strictly corrective. `skill-check check --help` exits 0 and prints the option list including `--no-security-scan`, so the corrected probe will now correctly match and resolve `{security_scan_flag} = "--no-security-scan"`, restoring the intended automated §2/§4 path. Risk consideration: if a *future/older* skill-check ever lacked the `check` subcommand, `check --help` could behave differently — but the live install proves `check --help` works and is the canonical location. The secondary `--skip-security` probe will still (correctly) miss because that flag genuinely does not exist in this version; behavior there is unchanged and the `--no-security-scan` primary probe now succeeds first. No breaking change.

# FINDING 2 (fp-0e8a9d6, gap) — ACCURATE

**Exact `rust-public-functions` recipe (extraction-patterns.md lines 296–305):**
```
296	**Rust — public functions:**
297	
298	```yaml
299	id: rust-public-functions
300	language: rust
301	rule:
302	  any:
303	    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET'
304	    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
305	```
```

**"Known ast-grep Limitations" section header:** line 382. Items #1–#7 occupy lines 386–401. Item #7 (lines 401) is the Python-class entry and is the correct style template to mirror:

> 7. **Python class patterns with bases/colon return zero (ast-grep 0.42.x):** The patterns `class $NAME($$$BASES)` and `class $NAME($$$BASES):` return zero matches on real Python sources with ast-grep 0.42.0, even on files containing dozens of subclassed public classes. `find_code_by_rule` also rejects the bare inline rule without `kind` as `Rule must specify a set of AST kinds to match. Try adding \`kind\` rule.` **Workaround:** Use the minimal `class $NAME` pattern with `kind: class_definition` (YAML) or `ast-grep run -p 'class $NAME' -l python --json=stream` (CLI), then post-filter names via the `^[^_]` regex. The `^[^_]` constraint enforces the "public" filter since ast-grep's base-match rule is what's broken, not the name-match rule. See the Python — public classes recipe above.

This entry is the right template: it states the version (`0.42.x`), the zero-match symptom, the over/under-match concern, a concrete workaround (minimal pattern + `kind` + regex post-filter), and a cross-reference back to the recipe.

**Confirmation there is no Rust entry:** Items #1–#7 cover: (#1) `find_code_by_rule` class kind, (#2) re-export multi-node, (#3) anonymous default exports, (#4) generic fallback protocol, (#5) TSX `export function` failure, (#6) CLI `--json=stream` empty, (#7) Python class bases/colon zero-match. None mentions Rust, `pub fn`, `pub(crate)`, or the `any:`-pattern zero-match. The finding's claim that no Rust entry exists is confirmed.

**Where to insert:** A new item **#8**, appended immediately after item #7 (after line 401, before the `### Component Library Demo/Example Auto-Exclusion` header at line 403). It is the last numbered list item, so the new entry slots in as #8 with no renumbering of existing items.

**Suggested new entry (mirroring #7's style; insert as item 8 after line 401):**
> 8. **Rust `pub fn` any-pattern returns zero; bare `pub fn $NAME` over-captures (ast-grep 0.42.x):** The `rust-public-functions` recipe's `any:` of `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET` / `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)` returns "No matches found" on real Rust sources with ast-grep 0.42.2, even on crates containing 200+ public functions. Dropping to the bare `pub fn $NAME` pattern over-captures restricted-visibility functions such as `pub(crate) fn`. **Workaround:** Use a visibility-constrained rule (e.g., constrain to bare `pub` without a `(...)` restriction) or fall back to a source grep (`rg '^\s*pub fn '` excluding `pub(`) at T1-low confidence. Never silently accept zero results for Rust public functions. See the Rust — public functions recipe above.

**Doc-only confirmation:** This is strictly an addition to the prose "Known ast-grep Limitations" documentation list. It changes no recipe YAML, no script, no workflow logic, and no executed command. The `rust-public-functions` recipe at lines 296–305 stays as-is; the new entry only documents the limitation and points readers to the workaround (exactly as #7 does for Python). No behavior change, no regression risk.

---

**Summary:** Both findings are ACCURATE. Finding 1 is a real functional bug reproduced against the live `skill-check` CLI (flags are on the `check` subcommand, not top-level help) affecting validate.md lines 64 and 68 only — minimal fix is inserting `check ` into both probe commands. Finding 2 is a real documentation gap — no Rust entry exists in "Known ast-grep Limitations"; the fix is a doc-only new item #8 appended after line 401, mirroring the Python item #7 template. No files were modified.

## FINDING 1 (fp-17da735, friction) — **PARTIALLY-ACCURATE**

**The defect is real, but the finding misstates the corrective convention and the "documented correct convention" location/wording.**

### What's accurate
All 9 listed frontmatter variables in `src/skf-brief-skill/` use the single hardcoded `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` path with no installed-path fallback:

| File | Line | Variable |
|---|---|---|
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/analyze-target.md` | 4 | `extractPublicApiScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/analyze-target.md` | 5 | `detectWorkspacesScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/analyze-target.md` | 6 | `detectLanguageScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/write-brief.md` | 6 | `writeSkillBriefScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/write-brief.md` | 7 | `emitBriefEnvelopeScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/write-brief.md` | 8 | `forgeTierRwScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/gather-intent.md` | 9 | `validateBriefInputsScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/gather-intent.md` | 10 | `emitBriefEnvelopeScript` |
| `src/skf-brief-skill/references/scope-definition.md` | 4 | `recommendScopeTypeScript` |

This breaks in installed SKF, where the module root is `_bmad/skf/` not `{project-root}/src/`.

### What's inaccurate / imprecise in the finding
1. **The cited evidence location is wrong.** The finding says the convention is at `_bmad/skf/shared/health-check.md:13`. That path does not exist — SKF is not installed under `_bmad/` in this repo (`_bmad/` contains bmb/bmm/cis only). The actual source-of-truth file is `src/shared/health-check.md`, and line 13 reads:
   > `> **Path convention:** This file is referenced as 'shared/health-check.md' from workflow step frontmatter. All 'shared/' paths resolve relative to the SKF module root ('_bmad/skf/' when installed, 'src/' during development), not relative to the calling step file.`

2. **The finding's implied fix (just swap to `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/`) is wrong** — that would break the dev checkout instead. The repo's actual established convention for resolving these scripts is a **dual-path probe list** (installed path first, `src/` dev fallback), e.g. `src/skf-quick-skill/references/resolve-target.md:4-6` and `src/skf-create-skill/references/report.md:6-8`:
   ```yaml
   atomicWriteProbeOrder:
     - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py'
     - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py'
   ```
   plus a step-body instruction to "probe in order, first existing path wins, HALT if neither resolves."

### Precise minimal fix
Each scalar variable must become a probe-order list. Example for `analyze-target.md:4-6`:

Before:
```yaml
extractPublicApiScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py'
detectWorkspacesScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py'
detectLanguageScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-language.py'
```
After:
```yaml
extractPublicApiScriptProbeOrder:
  - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py'
  - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py'
detectWorkspacesScriptProbeOrder:
  - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py'
  - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py'
detectLanguageScriptProbeOrder:
  - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-detect-language.py'
  - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-language.py'
```
(Same dual-path transformation for the other 6 variables across `write-brief.md` and `gather-intent.md` and `scope-definition.md`.)

### Regression / breaking-change risk
**Non-trivial.** Renaming variables (e.g. `extractPublicApiScript` → `…ProbeOrder`) means every `{extractPublicApiScript}` reference in the step bodies must be updated too, and the step bodies must gain explicit probe-and-resolve instructions (none exist today). A pure value swap (no rename) that just turns the scalar into a YAML list would break any `{var}` substitution that expects a single string. The correct fix is a coordinated frontmatter + step-body change per the established pattern — not a one-line edit. Lower-risk minimal alternative: keep scalar names but make body text resolve via probe order; still a multi-line change per file.

### Other src/ skills sharing this exact defect
`grep -rl '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts'` across `src/` (excluding `.analysis/` and `SKILL.md` noise) shows the **single-hardcoded-path** defect is present in **skf-brief-skill** and **skf-refine-architecture, skf-rename-skill, skf-setup, skf-update-skill, skf-verify-stack, skf-create-stack-skill, skf-drop-skill, skf-export-skill, skf-test-skill, skf-audit-skill, skf-analyze-source** (about **12 skills** beyond brief). Note: skf-quick-skill and skf-create-skill already use the correct dual-path probe pattern, so they appear in the grep only for their `src/` fallback line — they are NOT defective. (Per instructions, skf-analyze-source is covered by another agent.)

## FINDING 2 (fp-5604bab, bug) — **ACCURATE**

**Confirmed exactly as described.**

### Evidence
`src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py` argparse (lines 502–512) defines **only** `--mode` (with `choices=("quick",)`); there is **no `--language` argument**. Passing `--language <lang>` would cause `argparse` to error and exit non-zero.

Language is read from the **stdin JSON payload**, not a CLI flag:
- `src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py:514` `raw = sys.stdin.read()`
- `:519` `payload = json.loads(raw)`
- `:441` `language = (payload.get("language") or "").lower()`
- `:442-443` rejects unknown language values

The payload built in §4.1 step 2 already carries language — `src/skf-brief-skill/references/analyze-target.md:146`:
```json
"language": "<one of the supported values>",
```

### Exact before / after
File: `src/skf-brief-skill/references/analyze-target.md`, line **156** (inside §4.1 step 3 code fence at lines 153–157).

Before:
```bash
echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --language <lang> --mode quick
```
After:
```bash
echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --mode quick
```

(`--mode quick` is valid and harmless — it matches the argparse default and the payload's `"mode": "quick"`. Removing only `--language <lang>` is the minimal correct fix, matching the finding's suggestion.)

### Other invocations with --language
`grep -rn 'skf-extract-public-api' src/ --include=*.md | grep -- '--language'` (excluding `.analysis/`) returned **no other matches**. This is the only step file invoking the script with `--language`. (skf-create-skill is the other consumer of this script but is out of scope per instructions and was not flagged.)

### Regression / breaking-change risk
**None.** The flag is currently broken (argparse rejects it → non-zero exit → forces the §4.2 prose fallback for every script-supported language, defeating the script path entirely). Removing it makes the command actually work. If the variable still resolves to the broken `src/`-only path (Finding 1), the command will still fail to *locate* the script — Findings 1 and 2 are independent and both must be fixed for §4.1 to function. No callers depend on `--language` being accepted.

I have read the full file. The relevant sections are §2.1 (lines 38-44), §2.4 (lines 50-52), and the binding-severity line (line 27). Let me analyze.

## VERDICT: ACCURATE

The finding is accurate. The single-span limitation is real, no split-body carve-out exists, and the binding-severity rule prevents graceful handling.

---

### Evidence (exact current text)

**Binding severity rule — line 27:**
> `Perform the following explicit checks (no hand-waving — most use a single deterministic script; severity assignments are binding; do not relax them).`

**§2.1 anchor logic — lines 38–44.** §2.1 reads the `usage` family from the structural-scan JSON. Critically (line 42), the `usage` family is `satisfied: true` if **ANY** one synonym matches:
> line 42: `...the `tried[]` list from the JSON identifies which synonyms were checked: ... `Usage`/`Examples`/`How to use`/`Quickstart`/`Quick Start`/`Getting Started`/`Common Workflows` for usage...`

> line 44: `SKF-template skills' `## Quick Start`, `## Common Workflows`, and `## Key API Summary` are first-class synonyms baked into the script — they will surface with `satisfied: true` and the corresponding `matched_synonym` field.`

So §2.1's purpose is satisfaction (one match → pass). It produces a single `matched_synonym` — for an SKF split-body skill this is typically `## Quick Start`.

**§2.4 span-scan logic — lines 50–52 (exact current text):**
> line 50: `**2.4 Exports cross-used in Usage section.** For each function name reported in the step 3 subagent inventory (`exports[].name` where `kind == "function"` or `kind == "method"`):`
> line 51: `- `grep -c "{export.name}" SKILL.md` restricted to the Usage section (find the `## Usage` anchor from §2.1 and the next `^## ` anchor; count within that span).`
> line 52: `- **Zero occurrences → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — exported {kind} \`{name}\` is not referenced in the Usage section`. This catches the "documented but unused" failure mode that trivially fails discovery testing.`

---

### (a) Is the single-span limitation real? — YES

Line 51 instructs: "find the `## Usage` anchor from §2.1 and the next `^## ` anchor; count within that span." This is **exactly one** `## ` heading and the text up to the **next** `## ` heading — a single section span. For an SKF split-body skill, §2.1's matched anchor is `## Quick Start`, so the scan span is *only* the Quick Start block. Methods documented in `## Common Workflows`, `## Key API Summary`, or `references/*.md` fall outside that single span and produce `grep -c == 0` → **High severity** per line 52. The finding's "~25 false High findings on a verified 100%-coverage split-body skill" is a credible direct consequence of this logic.

A secondary problem confirms the finding: line 51 says "find the `## Usage` anchor" literally, but for SKF skills there is **no** `## Usage` heading at all (the matched synonym is `## Quick Start`). The prose doesn't tell the executor to use §2.1's `matched_synonym`; it hardcodes `## Usage`. This makes the single-span behavior even more brittle than the finding states.

### (b) Any existing split-body / usage-family handling §2.4 already has? — NONE

I read §§2.0–2.7 fully. §2.1 (line 44) *acknowledges* SKF split-body synonyms but only for the satisfaction check. §2.4 has **zero** awareness of: the usage-family union, `references/*.md`, `## Full` stubs, or split-body skills. No carve-out, no exception, no reference-file consultation exists anywhere in §2.4. The binding-severity rule (line 27) explicitly forbids relaxing the High, which is why the cited skill required a *documented deviation* rather than a clean pass. The finding did not miss any existing mitigation.

### (c) Minimal precise edit to §2.4

Replace lines 50–52. **Before:**
```
**2.4 Exports cross-used in Usage section.** For each function name reported in the step 3 subagent inventory (`exports[].name` where `kind == "function"` or `kind == "method"`):
- `grep -c "{export.name}" SKILL.md` restricted to the Usage section (find the `## Usage` anchor from §2.1 and the next `^## ` anchor; count within that span).
- **Zero occurrences → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — exported {kind} `{name}` is not referenced in the Usage section`. This catches the "documented but unused" failure mode that trivially fails discovery testing.
```

**After (proposed):**
```
**2.4 Exports cross-used in a usage-family section.** For each function name reported in the step 3 subagent inventory (`exports[].name` where `kind == "function"` or `kind == "method"`):
- Determine the usage-family search scope. Single-body skills: the span from §2.1's `matched_synonym` anchor to the next `^## ` anchor. Split-body skills (a `references/` directory exists alongside SKILL.md AND the SKILL.md `## Full*` sections are stubs/pointers): the union of EVERY usage-family heading present in SKILL.md (`Usage`/`Examples`/`How to use`/`Quickstart`/`Quick Start`/`Getting Started`/`Common Workflows`/`Key API Summary`), each from its anchor to the next `^## ` anchor, PLUS the full text of every file under `references/`.
- `grep -c "{export.name}"` across that scope and sum the counts.
- **Zero occurrences across the entire scope → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — exported {kind} `{name}` is not referenced in any usage-family section or reference file`. This catches the "documented but unused" failure mode that trivially fails discovery testing. A method referenced in any usage-family section OR any `references/` file satisfies the check.
```

This: (1) replaces the hardcoded `## Usage` with §2.1's actual `matched_synonym`, fixing the secondary bug; (2) adds the split-body union scope so 100%-coverage split-body skills no longer emit false Highs; (3) keeps the single-span behavior unchanged for ordinary single-body skills.

### (d) Regression risk — LOW, but one honest caveat

- **Does not weaken single-body detection:** the single-body branch is functionally unchanged (only the anchor-source bug is corrected). A genuinely undocumented export in a single-body skill still fails exactly as before.
- **Split-body masking risk:** the union scope means an export documented *anywhere* in any reference file passes §2.4. A bare *mention* of the name in any `references/*.md` (e.g. an unrelated narrative reference, a "see also", or a changelog entry) would satisfy the check. This is the same weakness §2.4 already has within a single span (`grep -c` counts any textual occurrence, not a real usage example), so the carve-out does not introduce a *new class* of weakness — it widens the existing one to N files. Net: §2.4 remains a coarse presence check, not a quality check; the carve-out trades ~25 false positives for a marginally higher false-negative surface. Given the finding documents a verified 100%-coverage skill being forced into a deviation, the trade is justified, but the masking caveat is real and should be acknowledged in the edit (the suggested-fix's "foregrounded-API" narrowing would tighten this further, at the cost of needing an API-foregrounding signal §2.4 does not currently have).

**No breaking change** to the JSON output schema (lines 67–77) — the `export_not_in_usage` finding type and shape are unaffected; only the detail string and trigger scope change.

## FINDING 1 (fp-8ea1d74) — **ACCURATE**

**Claim:** `identify-units.md` §2 Step D hardcodes `--source-root {project-root}`, wrong when the analyzed target is a remote repo or any path outside the SKF host project-root.

**Evidence:**
- `src/skf-analyze-source/references/identify-units.md:68`:
  `uv run {disqualifyCandidatesScript} filter --boundaries - --source-root {project-root}`
- `src/skf-analyze-source/references/identify-units.md:57` instructs paths be `"<rel-from-project-root>"` — same wrong anchor.
- `src/skf-analyze-source/references/init.md:55-64` explicitly accepts **remote URLs and arbitrary external paths** (`owner/repo`, `https://github.com/owner/repo2`, `/path/to/project`) and stores them in `project_paths[]` (init.md:71, :127).
- `scan-project.md:36` iterates `project_paths[]` (which may be external/remote) for the scan, and `scan-project.md:40` invokes `uv run {scanManifestsScript} scan {path}` — here it correctly passes `{path}` (the actual scanned root), **not** `{project-root}`. So the manifest scan in scan-project.md is already correct; the suggested fix's claim that scan-project.md §2 also needs the change is **INACCURATE on that sub-point** — scan-project.md:40 already uses `{path}` from `project_paths[]`.

**Resolution check — is there an existing resolved clone/source-root variable?** No.
- Grep for `forge-sources`, `analyzed_source`, `source_root`, `clone`, `git clone` across `src/skf-analyze-source/` returns **zero** clone/checkout logic. init.md:69 only says remote paths must be "accessible" — there is no documented step that clones a remote repo to a local working dir, and no `{analyzed_source_root}` / `.forge-sources/` variable exists anywhere in the workflow. The `.forge-sources/<lib>` path in the finding is hypothetical (it does not exist in the source).

**Precise minimal fix:**
The real bug is that `{project-root}` (the SKF host repo, from BMad config) is used as `--source-root` when the actual scanned root is an element of `project_paths[]`. Minimal change at `identify-units.md:68`:

- Before: `uv run {disqualifyCandidatesScript} filter --boundaries - --source-root {project-root}`
- After: `uv run {disqualifyCandidatesScript} filter --boundaries - --source-root {project_paths[0]}`

And `identify-units.md:57`: change `"path": "<rel-from-project-root>"` → `"path": "<rel-from-source-root (project_paths[0])>"` for consistency. (For genuine multi-repo, per-boundary source-root is the fuller fix, but single-root rebasing to `project_paths[0]` is the minimal correctness fix matching how scan-project.md:40 already behaves.)

**Regression/breaking-change risk:** Low. `disqualifyCandidatesScript` (`skf-disqualify-candidates.py`) takes `--source-root` as a filter base; pointing it at the actual scanned root makes file-count/LoC/generated-path detection correct. The boundaries JSON `path`/`files` (built in §2 from scan results) must then be relative to `project_paths[0]` rather than the SKF host root. No other file passes `--source-root` to this script (grep-confirmed: identify-units.md:68 is the only invocation). The remote-clone scenario in the finding cannot actually occur today (no clone logic), so the practical bug is the local-path-outside-SKF-root case.

## FINDING 2 (fp-d3ac68f) — **ACCURATE**

**Claim:** `outputFile: '{forge_data_folder}/analyze-source-report-{project_name}.md'` keys the report to the forge-workspace name, not the analyzed target; re-running for a different target collides and falsely triggers continuation-resume.

**Evidence:**
- `src/skf-analyze-source/references/init.md:4`: `outputFile: '{forge_data_folder}/analyze-source-report-{project_name}.md'`
- Same line replicated in: `scan-project.md:3`, `identify-units.md:3`, `map-and-detect.md:3`, `recommend.md:3`, `generate-briefs.md:2`, `continue.md:2` — **7 files** all derive the path from `{project_name}`.
- `{project_name}` is a BMad config-level variable loaded from project `config.yaml` (`SKILL.md:82` lists `project_name` among config-loaded vars alongside `output_folder`, `forge_data_folder`). It is the **SKF host/forge-workspace project name**, not the analyzed target. The analyzed target is `project_paths[]` (init.md:71/:127), which is independent of `{project_name}`.
- §1 resume logic, `init.md:24-31`:
  > "**IF the file exists AND has `stepsCompleted` with entries:** ... Load, read entirely, then execute {continueFile} — **STOP HERE**"
  - `continue.md` (Step 1b) then resumes from `stepsCompleted` and routes to the next step (continue.md:25-50) **without ever comparing the existing report's `project_paths` to the newly requested target.** So analyzing target B while a completed report for target A exists under the same `{project_name}` filename triggers a false resume of A's session.

**Minimal safe fix — risk assessment of the two options:**

The finding's "filename keyed to sanitized `project_paths[0]`" option is **higher risk**: the `outputFile` path is duplicated across 7 step files and is the contract between all of them, plus `templates/analysis-report-template.md` and the multi-repo "add a repo" flows (`recommend.md:168`, `map-and-detect.md:197`) that mutate `project_paths[]` mid-run — changing the filename basis to `project_paths[0]` would (a) require synchronized edits in all 7 files, and (b) break for multi-repo runs where `project_paths` has >1 entry or changes mid-session (the filename would no longer be stable within one analysis).

The **lower-risk fix is the §1 guard** (single-file change in `init.md`). Add to the `init.md:28-31` resume branch a precondition that the existing report's frontmatter `project_paths` matches the requested target before resuming:

- Before (init.md:28-31):
  ```
  **IF the file exists AND has `stepsCompleted` with entries:**
  - "**Found an existing analysis report. Resuming previous session...**"
  - Load, read entirely, then execute {continueFile}
  - **STOP HERE** — do not continue this sequence
  ```
- After:
  ```
  **IF the file exists AND has `stepsCompleted` with entries:**
  - Read the existing report frontmatter `project_paths`.
  - **IF existing `project_paths` does NOT match the requested target path(s):** the report belongs to a different analysis target. Do not resume — archive/rename the stale report (e.g. append a timestamp suffix) or overwrite it, then continue to section 2 as a fresh run.
  - **ELSE (same target):** "**Found an existing analysis report. Resuming previous session...**" — Load, read entirely, then execute {continueFile}. **STOP HERE** — do not continue this sequence.
  ```

Note this requires the target to be collected *before* the resume check, but init.md currently does the resume check in §1 and collects the path in §3 — so the guard also needs the requested target available at §1 (the `--project-path` headless flag is available at invocation per init.md:49; interactive path collection would need to move the prompt before/into the guard, or the guard prompts for the target first). This is a real ordering wrinkle the fix must address, but it is still contained to `init.md` and does not touch the 6 downstream files or the report-path contract that other consumers (`continue.md`, `recommend.md`, `generate-briefs.md`, template) depend on.

**Other consumers of the report path that the filename-change option would break:** `continue.md:2`, `scan-project.md:3`, `identify-units.md:3`, `map-and-detect.md:3`, `recommend.md:3`, `generate-briefs.md:2`, and `templates/analysis-report-template.md` (`{{project_name}}` at line 17). The guard option leaves all of these untouched → recommended.

## FINDING 3 (fp-f1b9560) — **PARTIALLY-ACCURATE** (premise is materially wrong; conclusion is over-narrow)

**Claim:** `scan-project.md:5` and `identify-units.md:5` point helper scripts at `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` which "doesn't exist in installed SKF" and `_bmad/skf/shared/health-check.md:13` documents the correct convention.

**What's INACCURATE:**

1. **Wrong path for the cited convention source.** There is **no** `_bmad/skf/shared/health-check.md` in this repo (the `_bmad/` tree here contains only `bmm`, `core`, `cis` — not `skf`). The file is at `src/shared/health-check.md`. Its line 13 reads (exact text):
   > `> **Path convention:** This file is referenced as `shared/health-check.md` from workflow step frontmatter. All `shared/` paths resolve relative to the SKF module root (`_bmad/skf/` when installed, `src/` during development), not relative to the calling step file.`
   - This convention is about the bare `shared/...` prefix resolving to module root. It does **not** prescribe a literal replacement string for the `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` form.

2. **Not a localized 2-file defect — it's the module-wide baseline.** `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` appears in frontmatter across **~25 reference files in 13+ skills** (full list below), not just the 2 cited. Treating scan-project.md:5 + identify-units.md:5 as an isolated defect mis-frames the issue for grouping.

3. **The documented correct fix is the probe-order pattern, not a single-string swap.** The established convention (`src/skf-quick-skill/references/finalize.md:1-6`, and 8+ other probe-order frontmatter blocks) is a two-element list, installed-first then dev-fallback:
   ```yaml
   atomicWriteProbeOrder:
     - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py'
     - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py'
   ```
   resolved at the use site by "first existing path wins" (finalize.md:32). So the `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` entries are not strictly *broken* — they are the dev-fallback half of a pattern that most files simply have not migrated to probe-order yet. Replacing them with a single `_bmad/skf/...` string would break the *development* tree (where `_bmad/skf/` does not exist), which is the inverse defect.

**What's ACCURATE:** scan-project.md:5 and identify-units.md:5 do single-path to `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` with no installed-path probe, so an installed SKF run (where only `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/` exists) would fail to resolve the helper — same latent friction the probe-order pattern was created to fix.

**Exact replacement (matching the documented/established convention):** Convert each single-string frontmatter key to a probe-order list, installed first:

- `scan-project.md:5`
  - Before: `scanManifestsScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-scan-manifests.py'`
  - After:
    ```yaml
    scanManifestsScriptProbeOrder:
      - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-scan-manifests.py'
      - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-scan-manifests.py'
    ```
    plus the use site `scan-project.md:40` must resolve `{scanManifestsScript}` from the probe-order list ("first existing path wins"), mirroring `compile.md:92`.
- `identify-units.md:5`
  - Before: `disqualifyCandidatesScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-disqualify-candidates.py'`
  - After: analogous `disqualifyCandidatesScriptProbeOrder` list + use-site resolution at `identify-units.md:68`.

**Full repo-wide list of files with `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts` in frontmatter/body (production `src/**`, excluding `.analysis/` reports):**

- `src/skf-analyze-source/references/scan-project.md:5`, `identify-units.md:5`
- `src/skf-quick-skill/references/quick-extract.md:5`, `resolve-target.md:6`, `write-and-validate.md:5,8`, `compile.md:6`, `finalize.md:5` (note: quick-skill *also* has the correct probe-order keys alongside — these are the dev-fallback entries)
- `src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/generate-output.md:11`, `detect-manifests.md:4`, `report.md:8`, `parallel-extract.md:3`, `detect-integrations.md:5`
- `src/skf-audit-skill/references/structural-diff.md:4,5`, `init.md:5,6`
- `src/skf-refine-architecture/references/init.md:12`
- `src/skf-verify-stack/references/init.md:4,21`, `report.md:9`, `coverage.md:5`, `requirements.md:4`, `integrations.md:6`, `synthesize.md:4`
- `src/skf-drop-skill/references/execute.md:13,20`
- `src/skf-export-skill/references/update-context.md:13,19,25`
- `src/skf-test-skill/references/coherence-check.md:7`, `init.md:12,74,83`, `report.md:17`
- `src/skf-create-skill/references/extract.md:14,21,31`, `validate.md:11,19,46`, `sub/fetch-docs.md:8`, `sub/fetch-temporal.md:8`, `generate-artifacts.md:10`, `report.md:8`, `load-brief.md:12`
- `src/skf-setup/references/write-config.md:12,18`, `auto-index.md:10,13`, `ccc-index.md:10`, `detect-and-tier.md:9`, `report.md:12`; `src/skf-setup/SKILL.md:58` (already documents the probe-order convention in prose)
- `src/skf-brief-skill/references/write-brief.md:6,7,8`, `analyze-target.md:4,5,6`, `scope-definition.md:4`, `gather-intent.md:9,10`
- `src/skf-update-skill/references/detect-changes.md:11,19,27`, `re-extract.md:14`, `write.md:11,19`
- `src/skf-rename-skill/references/execute.md:13,20,26,31`
- `src/shared/references/description-guard-protocol.md:18,42,88` (body, not frontmatter)

**Regression/grouping note:** Because this is the module-wide baseline (not a 2-file bug), the correct framing for grouping is a *single module-wide migration* to probe-order (the pattern `skf-quick-skill` and `skf-refine-architecture` already adopted), not an isolated scan-project/identify-units fix. Any single-string swap to `_bmad/skf/...` without keeping the `src/...` fallback would break the development tree and every dev/test run. The lowest-risk grouped fix is "migrate remaining single-string `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/...` frontmatter keys to two-element ProbeOrder lists with installed-path first," using `src/skf-quick-skill/references/finalize.md:1-6` as the canonical template.

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-export-skill/references/preflight-snippet-root-probe.md`

42	> **Snippet root prefix mismatch detected.**

47	>

61	
62	Halt and instruct the user to update `config.yaml` ... then re-run export. Exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"` ...
```

**Verification of the claim:** Confirmed accurate. The three options are exactly as described:
- (a) Set override → line 62 confirms **HALT, exit code 6, demands `config.yaml` edit + full re-run**.
- (b) Proceed → lines 64–66 confirm step 4 **rewrites every snippet's prefix to the reference IDE `skill_root`** (`.claude/skills/`), which in a correctly-structured authoring repo (snippets under `skills/`) produces a managed section pointing at paths that "don't resolve" (line 66 says exactly this).
- (c) Cancel → HALT.

There is indeed **no non-halt, non-mutating path** for a correctly-structured authoring repo on first export (when no manifest/snippets exist yet, the no-mismatch fast path at line 74 covers the empty case; but the friction is specifically the first export *where prior snippets already exist under `skills/`* and no override is set — the gate has no "use observed prefix for this run" path).

**Precise minimal fix:**

Insert a new option (d) in the gate block. After line 50 (`> - **(c) Cancel** — abort export and investigate.`), the bullet list should add a (d) before the closing blank line. Exact insertion — change:

```
> - **(c) Cancel** — abort export and investigate.
>
> If multiple distinct prefixes were observed, the snippets disagree with each other — investigate before choosing (a).
```
to:
```
> - **(c) Cancel** — abort export and investigate.
> - **(d) Use observed prefix for this run only** — set the effective snippet root to the single observed on-disk prefix for this export run without writing to `config.yaml`. The managed section will reference the real on-disk location. Persist as `snippet_skill_root_override: {observed_prefix}` to skip this prompt next time.
>
> If multiple distinct prefixes were observed, the snippets disagree with each other — investigate before choosing (a) or (d).
```

And add a choice-handling subsection. After line 70 (the `(c) Cancel` block ending `No files written.`) and before line 72 (`## No-mismatch fast path`), insert:

```
### (d) Use observed prefix for this run only

**Only available when `observed_prefixes` contains exactly one value.** Set the effective snippet root for this run to that single observed prefix (do not mutate `config.yaml`). Step 4's root-rewrite algorithm uses this value as the reference instead of `target_context_files[0].skill_root`, so snippets keep their on-disk prefix and the managed section resolves. Print a hint: "Persist `snippet_skill_root_override: {observed_prefix}` in config.yaml to skip this prompt on future exports." Continue to §2.
```

**Regression / contradiction assessment:** No contradiction.

- `src/skf-export-skill/assets/managed-section-format.md` defines the IDE→context-file→skill-root *mapping table*; it does not constrain the snippet on-disk prefix. Option (d) makes the managed section reference the *real* on-disk location, which is the same outcome option (a) achieves via `snippet_skill_root_override` (line 48 says the override makes "the managed section references the real location"). So (d) is functionally the in-run equivalent of (a) without the config write — semantically consistent with the override's existing contract documented in the frontmatter (lines 2–7: "when the override is set, every existing snippet's on-disk prefix is ground truth").
- Downstream consumer is step 4's root-rewrite algorithm, whose reference input is `target_context_files[0].skill_root` (line 34, line 66). Option (d) only redirects that single reference value for the run; it does not alter the manifest or `ides` semantics in update-context.md §9b. No breaking change to other steps.
- One precondition the suggested fix must enforce (and which the inserted text above handles): (d) is only sound when `observed_prefixes` has exactly one value — line 52 already flags multi-prefix as "investigate," so gating (d) on single-prefix is consistent with existing guidance.

378	3. **Generic parameter (deep fallback):** Search for `ComponentProps<typeof $NAME>` or `React.ComponentProps<typeof $NAME>` patterns that reference the component by name
379	
380	Unlinked Props interfaces are included as standalone type exports. Unlinked component exports are included with a note that no Props interface was found (signature-only, T1-low confidence for API contract).
381	
382	### Known ast-grep Limitations
383	
384	When using ast-grep for extraction, be aware of these documented limitations:
385	
386	1. **`find_code_by_rule` requires explicit `kind` for class exports:** The `export class $NAME` pattern needs a `kind` rule specifying the tree-sitter node type when used with `find_code_by_rule`. Use the simpler `find_code()` API instead for class detection.
387	
388	2. **Re-export patterns produce multiple AST nodes:** `export { A, B, C } from './module'` decomposes into multiple metavariable bindings for `$$$NAMES`. Results require post-processing to split comma-separated names.
389	
390	3. **Default anonymous exports capture no name:** `export default function $NAME` works, but `export default $EXPR` (anonymous default export) captures no name in `$NAME`. Fall back to source reading (T1-low) for anonymous defaults.
391	
392	4. **Fallback protocol:** If an ast-grep pattern returns errors or zero results when results are expected:
393	   - First: retry with `find_code()` using a simpler pattern (drop type annotations, use broader match)
394	   - Second: if `find_code()` also fails, fall back to source reading for that pattern category (T1-low confidence)
395	   - Never silently accept zero results for a pattern category that the source language commonly uses
396	
397	5. **TSX `export function` pattern failure:** The `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS)` pattern may return zero results in TSX files with ast-grep 0.41.x. This affects both MCP tools and CLI. `export const` and `export type` patterns are unaffected. **Workaround:** For TSX files, use `export const` patterns first (which work), then fall back to source reading (grep/file read) for `export function` declarations. When a TSX codebase shows zero `export function` matches but source files clearly contain them, this is a known ast-grep tree-sitter tsx parser limitation — not an extraction error. Log it in the evidence report and proceed with T1-low confidence for those exports.
398	
399	6. **CLI `--json=stream` may produce no output:** On ast-grep 0.41.x, `--json=stream` may produce empty output for certain patterns. The `--json=stream` flag requires the explicit `run` subcommand: use `ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' --json=stream` (not `ast-grep -p '{pattern}' --json=stream`). If streaming still produces no output, fall back to the MCP tool or source reading.
400	
401	7. **Python class patterns with bases/colon return zero (ast-grep 0.42.x):** The patterns `class $NAME($$$BASES)` and `class $NAME($$$BASES):` return zero matches on real Python sources with ast-grep 0.42.0, even on files containing dozens of subclassed public classes. `find_code_by_rule` also rejects the bare inline rule without `kind` as `Rule must specify a set of AST kinds to match. Try adding \`kind\` rule.` **Workaround:** Use the minimal `class $NAME` pattern with `kind: class_definition` (YAML) or `ast-grep run -p 'class $NAME' -l python --json=stream` (CLI), then post-filter names via the `^[^_]` regex. The `^[^_]` constraint enforces the "public" filter since ast-grep's base-match rule is what's broken, not the name-match rule. See the Python — public classes recipe above.
402	
403	### Component Library Demo/Example Auto-Exclusion
404	
405	When `scope.type: "component-library"`, auto-detect and propose demo/example exclusions before extraction begins. **User confirmation is required before applying** — some `examples/` directories contain API-level code.
406

43	> Existing snippets use: `{observed_prefixes}`
44	> IDE-mapped skill_root:  `{target_context_files[0].skill_root}`
45	>
46	> This usually means you are in an authoring repo where skills live under a single shared directory. Options:
47	>
48	> - **(a) Set override** — add `snippet_skill_root_override: {observed_prefix}` to `config.yaml`. Snippets keep their on-disk prefix; the managed section references the real location.
49	> - **(b) Proceed with IDE mapping** — step 4 will rewrite every snippet's root path to the IDE's skill_root. Use this only if the IDE's skill directory actually contains the skill files.
50	> - **(c) Cancel** — abort export and investigate.
51	>

58	## Choice handling
59	
60	### (a) Set override
61	
62	Halt and instruct the user to update `config.yaml` with `snippet_skill_root_override: {observed_prefix}`, then re-run export. Exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"` (configuration change is out-of-band — workflow does not edit config.yaml on the user's behalf).

65	
66	Continue to §2. Step 4's root-rewrite algorithm will rewrite every snippet's prefix to the reference `skill_root`. The user is accepting that the IDE's skill directory contains the actual files; if it does not, the resulting managed section will reference paths that don't resolve.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-create-skill/references/extraction-patterns.md",
  "old_string": "7. **Python class patterns with bases/colon return zero (ast-grep 0.42.x):** The patterns `class $NAME($$$BASES)` and `class $NAME($$$BASES):` return zero matches on real Python sources with ast-grep 0.42.0, even on files containing dozens of subclassed public classes. `find_code_by_rule` also rejects the bare inline rule without `kind` as `Rule must specify a set of AST kinds to match. Try adding \\`kind\\` rule.` **Workaround:** Use the minimal `class $NAME` pattern with `kind: class_definition` (YAML) or `ast-grep run -p 'class $NAME' -l python --json=stream` (CLI), then post-filter names via the `^[^_]` regex. The `^[^_]` constraint enforces the \"public\" filter since ast-grep's base-match rule is what's broken, not the name-match rule. See the Python — public classes recipe above.\n\n### Component Library Demo/Example Auto-Exclusion",
  "new_string": "7. **Python class patterns with bases/colon return zero (ast-grep 0.42.x):** The patterns `class $NAME($$$BASES)` and `class $NAME($$$BASES):` return zero matches on real Python sources with ast-grep 0.42.0, even on files containing dozens of subclassed public classes. `find_code_by_rule` also rejects the bare inline rule without `kind` as `Rule must specify a set of AST kinds to match. Try adding \\`kind\\` rule.` **Workaround:** Use the minimal `class $NAME` pattern with `kind: class_definition` (YAML) or `ast-grep run -p 'class $NAME' -l python --json=stream` (CLI), then post-filter names via the `^[^_]` regex. The `^[^_]` constraint enforces the \"public\" filter since ast-grep's base-match rule is what's broken, not the name-match rule. See the Python — public classes recipe above.\n\n8. **Rust `pub fn` any-pattern returns zero; bare `pub fn $NAME` over-captures (ast-grep 0.42.x):** The `rust-public-functions` recipe's `any:` of `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET` / `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)` returns \"No matches found\" on real Rust sources with ast-grep 0.42.2, even on crates containing 200+ public functions. Dropping to the bare `pub fn $NAME` pattern matches, but over-captures restricted-visibility functions such as `pub(crate) fn` / `pub(super) fn`, which are **not** public API. **Workaround:** Prefer a visibility-constrained source grep — `rg '^\\s*pub fn ' <src>` filtered to exclude lines beginning `pub(` — cross-checked against the AN-verified public surface, at T1-low confidence. Never silently accept zero results for Rust public functions, and never treat a bare `pub fn $NAME` match set as the public API without stripping `pub(...)`-restricted items. See the Rust — public functions recipe above.\n\n### Component Library Demo/Example Auto-Exclusion"
}
```

This catches the \"documented but unused\" failure mode that trivially fails discovery testing.",
  "new_string": "**2.4 Exports cross-used in a usage-family section.** For each function name reported in the step 3 subagent inventory (`exports[].name` where `kind == \"function\"` or `kind == \"method\"`):\n- Determine the usage-family search scope:\n  - **Single-body skill** (no `references/` directory, or `## Full*` sections carry real content): the span from §2.1's `matched_synonym` anchor to the next `^## ` anchor.\n  - **Split-body skill** (a `references/` directory exists alongside SKILL.md AND the SKILL.md `## Full*` sections are stubs/pointers): the union of EVERY usage-family heading present in SKILL.md (`Usage`/`Examples`/`How to use`/`Quickstart`/`Quick Start`/`Getting Started`/`Common Workflows`/`Key API Summary`), each from its anchor to the next `^## ` anchor, PLUS the full text of every file under `references/`.\n- `grep -c \"{export.name}\"` across that scope and sum the counts.\n- **Zero occurrences across the entire scope → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — exported {kind} \\`{name}\\` is not referenced in any usage-family section or reference file`. This catches the \"documented but unused\" failure mode that trivially fails discovery testing. A method referenced in any usage-family section OR any `references/` file satisfies the check."

12	Commands:

16	  remove <skill-name>           — Remove skill from manifest
17	  deprecate <skill-name> [ver]  — Mark skill or version as deprecated
18	  rename <old-name> <new-name>  — Rename a skill entry
19	"""

214	
215	    if command == "read":
216	        result = cmd_read(manifest_path)
217	    elif command == "get" and len(sys.argv) >= 4:
218	        result = cmd_get(manifest_path, sys.argv[3])

222	        result = cmd_remove(manifest_path, sys.argv[3])

225	        result = cmd_deprecate(manifest_path, sys.argv[3], ver)
226	    elif command == "rename" and len(sys.argv) >= 5:
227	        result = cmd_rename(manifest_path, sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4])
228	    else:
229	        result = {"status": "error", "error": f"Unknown command or missing args: {command}"}
230	
231	    print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
232	    sys.exit(0 if result["status"] == "ok" else 1)

When\n    # --ides is supplied, union it in (dedupe, keep sorted).\n    existing_version = versions.get(version, {})\n    merged_ides = list(existing_version.get(\"ides\", existing_version.get(\"platforms\", [])))\n    if ides:\n        for ide in ides:\n            if ide not in merged_ides:\n                merged_ides.append(ide)\n        merged_ides = sorted(merged_ides)\n    versions[version] = {\n        \"ides\": merged_ides,\n        \"last_exported\": today,\n        \"status\": \"active\",\n    }"

308	
309	**This section executes ONCE after all context-file iterations complete** (outside the per-context-file loop defined in section 3). Only IDEs whose target context files were successfully written and verified in section 9 are recorded.
310	
311	**`ides` field definition:** `ides` is the list of IDE identifiers from `config.yaml.ides` (e.g. `claude-code`, `cursor`, `github-copilot`) whose context files were successfully written and verified in section 9. It is NOT the context file name (`CLAUDE.md`) and NOT the skill root path (`.claude/skills/`). Each IDE → context file → skill root mapping is defined in `skf-export-skill/assets/managed-section-format.md`.
312	
313	1. Read `{skills_output_folder}/.export-manifest.json` (or start with `{"schema_version": "2", "exports": {}}` if it does not exist)
314	2. Ensure `schema_version` is `"2"` (if v1 was migrated in section 4a, the migrated structure is already in context). If any version entry still has a legacy `platforms` key, rename it to `ides` in place (see §4a).
315	3. Compute `ides_written` — the set of IDE identifiers from `config.yaml.ides` whose mapped context file was successfully written in section 9 (deduplicated, sorted). When `--context-file` was passed explicitly, `ides_written` contains only the IDEs that map to that single context file.
316	4. For each skill in `skill_batch` (multi-skill mode) — or the single current skill (single-skill mode) — add or update its entry in v2 format:
317	   ```json
318	   "{skill-name}": {
319	     "active_version": "{version}",
320	     "versions": {
321	       "{version}": {
322	         "ides": ["{ides_written}"],
323	         "last_exported": "{current-date}",
324	         "status": "active"
325	       }
326	     }
327	   }
328	   ```
329	   - `{version}` is the version from each skill's `{resolved_skill_package}/metadata.json`
330	   - If the skill already has a manifest entry:
331	     - Set `active_version` to the current version
332	     - If the version already exists in `versions`, union its existing `ides` with `ides_written` (deduplicate, keep sorted), refresh `last_exported`, and set `status: "active"`
333	     - If this is a new version, add it to `versions` with `status: "active"` and set any previously-active version's status to `"archived"`
334	     - Preserve all other version entries in `versions` (do not delete archived versions)
335	5. Write the updated manifest atomically via `{manifestOpsHelper}` after all skills in the batch have been applied. For each skill / version pair, invoke:
336	
337	   ```bash
338	   python3 {manifestOpsHelper} {skills_output_folder} set {skill-name} {version}
339	   ```
340	
341	   The helper handles v2-schema validation, v1→v2 migration, and `platforms`→`ides` rename internally — no in-prompt JSON manipulation needed. Each `set` invocation merges the per-version `ides` and `last_exported` fields into the existing entry. After all skills have been set, re-read the manifest via `{manifestOpsHelper} {skills_output_folder} read` to confirm the final state matches expectations.
342	
343	**Dry-run mode:** Do NOT update the manifest. Display: "**[DRY RUN] Export manifest would be updated for {skill-name-list} — ides: {ides_written}.**" (list every skill in `skill_batch`)
```

28	**IF the file exists AND has `stepsCompleted` with entries:**
29	- "**Found an existing analysis report. Resuming previous session...**"
30	- Load, read entirely, then execute {continueFile}
31	- **STOP HERE** — do not continue this sequence
32	
33	**IF the file does not exist OR stepsCompleted is empty:**
34	- Continue to section 2
35	
36	### 2. Verify Prerequisites
37	
38	**Check forge-tier.yaml:**
39	- Look for `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`
40	- **IF missing:** HARD HALT — "**Cannot proceed.** forge-tier.yaml not found at `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`. Please run the setup workflow first to configure your forge tier (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep)."
41	- **IF found:** Read and note the forge tier value
42	
43	**Apply tier override:** Read `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml`. If `tier_override` is set and is a valid tier value (Quick, Forge, Forge+, or Deep), use it instead of the detected tier.
44	
45	"**Forge tier detected:** {tier} — analysis depth will be calibrated accordingly."
46	
47	### 3. Collect Project Path
48	
49	**Headless flag consumption:** If `--project-path <path>` was passed at invocation, set `project_paths[]` directly from the flag value (comma-split if multiple paths were supplied), skip the prompt below, and proceed to validation. Otherwise prompt as today.
50	
51	"**Welcome to Analyze Source — the SKF decomposition engine.**
52	
53	I'll analyze your project to identify discrete skillable units and produce skill-brief.yaml files for each recommended unit.
54	
55	**Please provide the project root path(s) to analyze:**
56	
57	This can be:
58	- A single root directory of a repo or multi-service project
59	- Multiple paths or URLs (comma-separated) for multi-repo analysis (e.g., integration/stack skills)
60	
61	Examples:
62	- `/path/to/project`
63	- `owner/repo, owner/repo2`
64	- `/path/to/project, https://github.com/owner/repo2`"
65	
66	Wait for user input.
67	
68	**Validate the path(s):**
69	- For each provided path/URL: check that it exists (local) or is accessible (remote)
70	- **IF any invalid:** "Path `{path}` doesn't appear to be valid. Please correct it."
71	- Store as `project_paths[]` array in report frontmatter (single path stored as 1-element array for consistency)
72	
73	**Collect intent hint** (drives recommendation ranking in Step 5):
74	
75	**Headless flag consumption:** If `--intent-hint <text>` was passed at invocation, set workflow-context `intent_hint` directly from the flag value, skip the prompt below, and proceed. If `{headless_mode}` is true and no `--intent-hint` was supplied, set `intent_hint = ""` (empty) and proceed without prompting.

{

  "old_string": "        r = mod.cmd_get(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\")\n        entry = r[\"entry\"][\"versions\"][\"2.0.0\"]\n        assert entry[\"ides\"] == [\"cursor\"]\n        assert \"platforms\" not in entry",
  "new_string": "        r = mod.cmd_get(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\")\n        entry = r[\"entry\"][\"versions\"][\"2.0.0\"]\n        assert entry[\"ides\"] == [\"cursor\"]\n        assert \"platforms\" not in entry\n\n    def test_set_with_ides_writes_sorted_deduped(self, manifest_path):\n        \"\"\"S15: cmd_set(ides=...) on a new version records the deduped, sorted list.\"\"\"\n        mod.cmd_set(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\", \"2.0.0\", ides=[\"cursor\", \"claude-code\", \"cursor\"])\n        r = mod.cmd_get(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\")\n        entry = r[\"entry\"][\"versions\"][\"2.0.0\"]\n        assert entry[\"ides\"] == [\"claude-code\", \"cursor\"]\n\n    def test_set_with_ides_unions_existing(self, manifest_path):\n        \"\"\"S16: cmd_set(ides=...) unions into the version's existing ides; absence preserves.\"\"\"\n        mod.cmd_set(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\", \"2.0.0\", ides=[\"claude-code\"])\n        # Re-set same version with a new IDE → union, deduped + sorted\n        mod.cmd_set(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\", \"2.0.0\", ides=[\"cursor\", \"claude-code\"])\n        entry = mod.cmd_get(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\")[\"entry\"][\"versions\"][\"2.0.0\"]\n        assert entry[\"ides\"] == [\"claude-code\", \"cursor\"]\n        # Re-set with no --ides → existing list preserved verbatim (backward-compatible)\n        mod.cmd_set(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\", \"2.0.0\")\n        entry = mod.cmd_get(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\")[\"entry\"][\"versions\"][\"2.0.0\"]\n        assert entry[\"ides\"] == [\"claude-code\", \"cursor\"]\n\n    def test_cli_set_parses_ides_flag(self, manifest_path):\n        \"\"\"S17: the `set ... --ides a,b` CLI dispatch path parses and unions the list.\"\"\"\n        script = Path(__file__).parent.parent / \"src\" / \"shared\" / \"scripts\" / \"skf-manifest-ops.py\"\n        proc = subprocess.run(\n            [sys.executable, str(script), str(manifest_path.parent),\n             \"set\", \"cocoindex\", \"2.0.0\", \"--ides\", \"cursor,claude-code\"],\n            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30,\n        )\n        assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stderr\n        entry = mod.cmd_get(manifest_path, \"cocoindex\")[\"entry\"][\"versions\"][\"2.0.0\"]\n        assert entry[\"ides\"] == [\"claude-code\", \"cursor\"]"
}

3	- [CHANGELOG.md is release-managed](feedback_changelog_managed_by_release.md) — Never hand-edit CHANGELOG.md; it's regenerated by the release workflow from commits/PRs
4	- [No inline version-stamp markers in user docs](feedback_no_inline_version_stamps_in_docs.md) — Don't write `(vX.Y.Z+)` / "Added in vX.Y.Z" inline in `docs/`/`README`; CHANGELOG is the canonical "when added" record

### CANONICAL TEMPLATE TO REUSE (simplest established phrasing)

The minimal established phrasing is the **`finalize.md` single-line form**. For each migrated variable, the rename target is `<baseName>Helper` (drop `Script`/`Path`, do not rename `*Helper`) and the new frontmatter key is `<baseName>ProbeOrder`.

**Frontmatter (replace the scalar with):**
```yaml
<base>ProbeOrder:
  - '{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/<script>.py'
  - '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/<script>.py'
```

**Body resolution instruction (add once, near first use of the helper):**
> **Resolve `{<base>Helper}`** from `{<base>ProbeOrder}`; first existing path wins. HALT if no candidate exists.

(Use the optional cause-specific tail "— …" only when there is a known regression to call out, mirroring `finalize.md`. Use the `resolve-target.md` "If no candidate exists, fall back to …" variant only where a documented prose fallback path already exists.)

All existing body usages of `{currentVarName}` (which invoke `python3 {var} …` or `uv run {var} …`) get the variable token renamed `{currentVarName}` → `{<base>Helper}`; the command form is unchanged.

## PART 4 — Risk Notes

1. **No true duplicate-script collisions.** `src/skf-verify-stack/references/init.md` is the only file with BOTH a defective scalar (`atomicWriteScript`, line 4) AND a correct ProbeOrder key (`enumerateStackSkillsProbeOrder`, lines 19–21). They reference *different* scripts (`skf-atomic-write.py` vs `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py`), so adding `atomicWriteProbeOrder` will NOT create a duplicate key. No file pairs a defective scalar with a ProbeOrder for the *same* script.

2. **`emitBriefEnvelopeScript` declared twice (gather-intent.md:10, write-brief.md:7).** These are two independent step files each owning their own copy — correct and expected (step files are loaded independently). Both must migrate to `emitBriefEnvelopeProbeOrder` / `{emitBriefEnvelopeHelper}`, each with its own body resolution instruction. No body `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` usage exists in gather-intent.md itself (only the frontmatter declaration) — verify whether that declaration is dead/unused before migrating, or whether it is consumed by a reference loaded from gather-intent.md.

112	def _run(cmd: list[str], timeout: int = PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC) -> tuple[int, str, str]:
113	    """Run a subprocess. Return (returncode, stdout, stderr). Never raises.
114	
115	    Treats every failure mode (FileNotFoundError, TimeoutExpired, OSError,
116	    CalledProcessError) as a failed probe — returns rc=127 and an empty
117	    stdout/stderr. Tool detection should never crash the workflow.
118	    """

120	        result = subprocess.run(

122	            capture_output=True,
123	            text=True,
124	            timeout=timeout,
125	            check=False,
126	        )
127	        return result.returncode, result.stdout or "", result.stderr or ""
128	    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):
129	        return 127, "", ""

136	        if stripped:
137	            return stripped

171	def probe_ccc() -> dict:
172	    """Two-step probe: --help with identity marker, then doctor for daemon health."""
173	    rc, stdout, _ = _run(["ccc", "--help"])
174	    if rc != 0:
175	        return {"available": False, "daemon": None, "version": None}
176	    if CCC_IDENTITY_MARKER not in stdout.lower():
177	        # `ccc` resolved to a foreign binary (e.g. code2prompt alias). Refuse.
178	        return {"available": False, "daemon": None, "version": None}
179	
180	    rc_doctor, doctor_stdout, _ = _run(["ccc", "doctor"])
181	    version = _first_line(doctor_stdout) or _first_line(stdout)
182	    if rc_doctor == 0:
183	        return {"available": True, "daemon": "healthy", "version": version}
184	    # Distinguishing "stopped" from "error" requires parsing doctor output;
185	    # without a documented contract, default to "error" and let the caller

195	def calculate_tier(tools: dict) -> str:
196	    ag = tools["ast_grep"]["available"]
197	    gh = tools["gh_cli"]["available"]
198	    qm = tools["qmd"]["available"]
199	    cc = tools["ccc"]["available"]
200	
201	    if ag and gh and qm:
202	        return "Deep"
203	    if ag and cc:

205	    if ag:
206	        return "Forge"
207	    return "Quick"

209	
210	def suggest_valid_tier(bad_value: str) -> str | None:
211	    """For an invalid --tier-override value, return the closest valid tier name.
212	
213	    Two-stage match:
214	    1. Case-insensitive exact match — handles `deep` / `DEEP` / `forge+` /
215	       `FORGE+` (the most common typo class: right tier, wrong case).
216	    2. difflib fuzzy match against `VALID_TIERS` with a 0.6 cutoff — handles
217	       `frorge`, `quik`, `forge plus`, etc.
218	
219	    Returns None if no candidate clears the cutoff. Used only for diagnostic
220	    messages — the override itself is never silently auto-corrected.
221	    """
222	    import difflib
223	
224	    if not bad_value or not isinstance(bad_value, str):
225	        return None
226	    cleaned = bad_value.strip()
227	    if not cleaned:
228	        return None
229	    cleaned_lower = cleaned.lower()
230	    for valid in VALID_TIERS:
231	        if valid.lower() == cleaned_lower:
232	            return valid
233	    matches = difflib.get_close_matches(cleaned, VALID_TIERS, n=1, cutoff=0.6)
234	    return matches[0] if matches else None
235	
236	
237	def tier_prerequisites_met(tier: str, tools: dict) -> tuple[bool, list[str]]:
238	    """Return (satisfied, missing_tools) for tier-prerequisite checks.
239	
240	    Used by both --tier-override sanity check and --require-tier evaluation.
241	    Deep does NOT subsume Forge+ (Deep does not require ccc), so a Deep
242	    calculation with no ccc still fails a Forge+ requirement.
243	    """
244	    needed: dict[str, str] = {
245	        "Quick": {},
246	        "Forge": {"ast_grep": "ast-grep"},
247	        "Forge+": {"ast_grep": "ast-grep", "ccc": "ccc"},
248	        "Deep": {"ast_grep": "ast-grep", "gh_cli": "gh", "qmd": "qmd"},
249	    }[tier]
250	    missing = [display for key, display in needed.items() if not tools[key]["available"]]
251	    return (len(missing) == 0, missing)
252	
253	
254	def read_prior_state(prior_state_path) -> dict:
255	    """Read forge-tier.yaml from a previous run, return prior tier / tools / detection_date.
256	
257	    Returns a flat dict (always present, keys always set) so callers don't
258	    branch on missing-file vs empty-file vs malformed-file — the script owns
259	    that classification. On any read failure or absent file, returns the
260	    "first-run" shape (everything null/empty). The CCC freshness fields are
261	    surfaced separately so the caller doesn't reparse YAML in prose.
262	    """
263	    empty = {
264	        "previous_tier": None,
265	        "previous_detection_date": None,
266	        "previous_tools": {},
267	        "previous_ccc_index_status": None,
268	        "previous_ccc_indexed_path": None,
269	        "previous_ccc_last_indexed": None,
270	        "previous_ccc_staleness_threshold_hours": None,
271	    }
272	    if not prior_state_path:
273	        return empty
274	    try:
275	        import yaml  # local import — only needed when --prior-state-from is used
276	        from pathlib import Path as _P
277	        p = _P(prior_state_path)
278	        if not p.exists():
279	            return empty
280	        data = yaml.safe_load(p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) or {}
281	    except Exception:
282	        return empty
283	
284	    tools_map = data.get("tools") if isinstance(data.get("tools"), dict) else {}
285	    ccc_index = data.get("ccc_index") if isinstance(data.get("ccc_index"), dict) else {}
286	
287	    return {
288	        "previous_tier": data.get("tier") if data.get("tier") in VALID_TIERS else None,
289	        "previous_detection_date": data.get("tier_detected_at"),
290	        "previous_tools": tools_map,
291	        "previous_ccc_index_status": ccc_index.get("status"),
292	        "previous_ccc_indexed_path": ccc_index.get("indexed_path"),
293	        "previous_ccc_last_indexed": ccc_index.get("last_indexed"),
294	        "previous_ccc_staleness_threshold_hours": ccc_index.get("staleness_threshold_hours"),
295	    }
296	
297

> AGENT

Root cause isolated: **`qmd status` hangs** (killed only by SIGKILL at 14s; it spawns a daemon grandchild). `_run()` uses `subprocess.run(capture_output=True, timeout=8)` — on timeout Python kills the direct child but blocks draining pipes the escaped daemon still holds, so the 8s timeout is never enforced → `probe_qmd()` hangs → script runs >90s → test's 30s timeout fails.

Before fixing `_run()`, I must check how the existing tests mock it (changing the impl must not break the 38 passing tests):

> TOOL

> AGENT

The fix must keep `subprocess.run` as the call (tests mock it by return-value) while genuinely bounding wall-time. Root cause is the post-timeout **pipe drain** blocking on the escaped `qmd` daemon. Redirecting child stdio to temp files instead of pipes eliminates the drain (so `subprocess.run`'s timeout is actually enforced) and is transparent to the return-value mocks. Implementing:

> TOOL

tool_use Read

112	def _run(cmd: list[str], timeout: int = PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC) -> tuple[int, str, str]:
113	    """Run a subprocess. Return (returncode, stdout, stderr). Never raises.
114	
115	    Treats every failure mode (FileNotFoundError, TimeoutExpired, OSError,
116	    CalledProcessError) as a failed probe — returns rc=127 and an empty
117	    stdout/stderr. Tool detection should never crash the workflow.
118	    """
119	    try:
120	        result = subprocess.run(
121	            cmd,
122	            capture_output=True,
123	            text=True,
124	            timeout=timeout,
125	            check=False,
126	        )
127	        return result.returncode, result.stdout or "", result.stderr or ""
128	    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):
129	        return 127, "", ""
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-tools.py",
  "old_string": "def _run(cmd: list[str], timeout: int = PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC) -> tuple[int, str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Run a subprocess. Return (returncode, stdout, stderr). Never raises.\n\n    Treats every failure mode (FileNotFoundError, TimeoutExpired, OSError,\n    CalledProcessError) as a failed probe — returns rc=127 and an empty\n    stdout/stderr. Tool detection should never crash the workflow.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            cmd,\n            capture_output=True,\n            text=True,\n            timeout=timeout,\n            check=False,\n        )\n        return result.returncode, result.stdout or \"\", result.stderr or \"\"\n    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):\n        return 127, \"\", \"\"",
  "new_string": "def _run(cmd: list[str], timeout: int = PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC) -> tuple[int, str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Run a subprocess. Return (returncode, stdout, stderr). Never raises.\n\n    Treats every failure mode (FileNotFoundError, TimeoutExpired, OSError,\n    CalledProcessError) as a failed probe — returns rc=127 and an empty\n    stdout/stderr. Tool detection should never crash the workflow.\n\n    Child stdout/stderr are redirected to temp files rather than captured via\n    OS pipes. A daemon-backed probe (e.g. `qmd status`) forks a long-lived\n    grandchild that inherits the captured pipe's write end; with\n    `capture_output=True`, `subprocess.run`'s post-timeout drain then blocks\n    forever on that surviving FD and the `timeout` is never enforced. File\n    redirection has no pipe to drain, so `timeout` is honoured even when the\n    tool leaves a daemon behind, and `start_new_session=True` isolates it.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        with tempfile.TemporaryFile() as out_f, tempfile.TemporaryFile() as err_f:\n            result = subprocess.run(\n                cmd,\n                stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,\n                stdout=out_f,\n                stderr=err_f,\n                timeout=timeout,\n                check=False,\n                start_new_session=True,\n            )\n            # Mocked unit tests patch `subprocess.run` to return a fake with\n            # `.stdout`/`.stderr` strings set; real runs redirect to the temp\n            # files (so `result.stdout` is None — read the files instead).\n            stdout = result.stdout\n            if stdout is None:\n                out_f.seek(0)\n                stdout = out_f.read().decode(\"utf-8\", \"replace\")\n            stderr = result.stderr\n            if stderr is None:\n                err_f.seek(0)\n                stderr = err_f.read().decode(\"utf-8\", \"replace\")\n            return result.returncode, stdout or \"\", stderr or \"\"\n    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):\n        return 127, \"\", \"\""
}

> AGENT

Now I have hard evidence. The hang is **`subprocess.run`'s own post-timeout `process.wait()`** (subprocess.py:1280→2066→2024, called from `run()`:569) inside `probe_qmd`'s `qmd status`. `subprocess.run` raises TimeoutExpired at 8s, sends SIGKILL, then calls an **unbounded** `process.wait()` — but `qmd status` is blocked on a daemon socket (D-state), so SIGKILL is deferred and that wait never returns → `fut.result()` blocks forever.

The robust, mock-compatible fix: wrap the child in the OS `timeout(1)` utility (with `-k`) so wall-time is bounded at the OS level regardless of Python's post-timeout reaping. `subprocess.run` stays the mocked primitive (tests patch it; the wrapper only affects real runs).

> TOOL

A daemon-backed\n# tool (e.g. `qmd status`) can block in an uninterruptible syscall against\n# its daemon; `subprocess.run`'s own post-timeout `process.wait()` is\n# unbounded and never returns in that case, hanging the whole detector.\n# `timeout --kill-after` reaps the child (and its session) at the OS level,\n# so Python's wait always returns. None on platforms without it (then we\n# fall back to subprocess.run's own timeout, accepting the rare hang).\n_TIMEOUT_BIN = shutil.which(\"timeout\")"
