```
122	def _run(cmd: list[str], timeout: int = PROBE_TIMEOUT_SEC) -> tuple[int, str, str]:
123	    """Run a subprocess. Return (returncode, stdout, stderr). Never raises.
124	
125	    Treats every failure mode (FileNotFoundError, TimeoutExpired, OSError,
126	    CalledProcessError) as a failed probe — returns rc=127 and an empty
127	    stdout/stderr. Tool detection should never crash the workflow.
128	
129	    Child stdout/stderr are redirected to temp files rather than captured via
130	    OS pipes. A daemon-backed probe (e.g. `qmd status`) forks a long-lived
131	    grandchild that inherits the captured pipe's write end; with
132	    `capture_output=True`, `subprocess.run`'s post-timeout drain then blocks
133	    forever on that surviving FD and the `timeout` is never enforced. File
134	    redirection has no pipe to drain, so `timeout` is honoured even when the
135	    tool leaves a daemon behind, and `start_new_session=True` isolates it.
136	    """
137	    try:
138	        with tempfile.TemporaryFile() as out_f, tempfile.TemporaryFile() as err_f:
139	            result = subprocess.run(
140	                cmd,
141	                stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,
142	                stdout=out_f,
143	                stderr=err_f,
144	                timeout=timeout,
145	                check=False,
146	                start_new_session=True,
147	            )
148	            # Mocked unit tests patch `subprocess.run` to return a fake with
149	            # `.stdout`/`.stderr` strings set; real runs redirect to the temp
150	            # files (so `result.stdout` is None — read the files instead).
151	            stdout = result.stdout
152	            if stdout is None:
153	                out_f.seek(0)
154	                stdout = out_f.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
155	            stderr = result.stderr
156	            if stderr is None:
157	                err_f.seek(0)
158	                stderr = err_f.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
159	            return result.returncode, stdout or "", stderr or ""
160	    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):
161	        return 127, "", ""
162

165	    """Return the first non-empty stripped line of `text`, or None."""
166	    for line in text.splitlines():
```

```
def _fake_run(rc: int = 0, stdout: str = "", stderr: str = ""):
    """Build a CompletedProcess-like object _run() can wrap."""
    class _Result:
        def __init__(self):
            self.returncode = rc
            self.stdout = stdout
            self.stderr = stderr
    return _Result()
    )
    doctor_output = "All systems operational\n"

    def _side_effect(cmd, **kwargs):
        if cmd == ["ccc", "--help"]:
            return _fake_run(0, help_output)
        if cmd == ["ccc", "doctor"]:
            return _fake_run(0, doctor_output)
        raise AssertionError(f"unexpected probe call: {cmd}")

    with patch.object(mod.subprocess, "run", side_effect=_side_effect):
        result = mod.probe_ccc()
    assert result["available"] is True
    assert result["daemon"] == "healthy"


def test_probe_ccc_identity_marker_absent_rejects_alias():
    """Foreign `ccc` binary (e.g. code2prompt alias) must not pass."""
    # Foreign tool exits 0 on --help but lacks the marker
    foreign_help = "Usage: ccc [OPTIONS]\n\nA generic tool that happens to use ccc as its name.\n"

    def _side_effect(cmd, **kwargs):
        if cmd == ["ccc", "--help"]:
            return _fake_run(0, foreign_help)
        raise AssertionError(
            "ccc doctor MUST NOT be called when identity marker is absent — "
            f"called with {cmd}"
        )

    with patch.object(mod.subprocess, "run", side_effect=_side_effect):
        result = mod.probe_ccc()
    assert result == {"available": False, "daemon": None, "version": None}


def test_probe_qmd_binary_present_daemon_stopped():
    def _side_effect(cmd, **kwargs):
        if cmd == ["qmd", "--version"]:
            return _fake_run(0, "qmd 1.2.3\n")
        if cmd == ["qmd", "status"]:
            return _fake_run(1, "", "qmd: daemon not running\n")
        raise AssertionError(f"unexpected: {cmd}")

    with patch.object(mod.subprocess, "run", side_effect=_side_effect):
        result = mod.probe_qmd()
    assert result == {"available": False, "status": "daemon_stopped", "version": "qmd 1.2.3"}


def test_probe_qmd_falls_back_to_help_when_version_unsupported():
    """Some qmd builds reject --version; fall back to --help for identity check."""
    def _side_effect(cmd, **kwargs):
        if cmd == ["qmd", "--version"]:
            return _fake_run(2, "", "unknown option --version\n")
        if cmd == ["qmd", "--help"]:
            return _fake_run(0, "qmd: a search engine\n")
        if cmd == ["qmd", "status"]:
            return _fake_run(0, "Operational\n")
        raise AssertionError(f"unexpected: {cmd}")

    with patch.object(mod.subprocess, "run", side_effect=_side_effect):
        result = mod.probe_qmd()
    assert result["available"] is True
    assert result["status"] == "healthy"


def test_probe_qmd_absent():
    with patch.object(mod.subprocess, "run", side_effect=FileNotFoundError):
        result = mod.probe_qmd()
    assert result == {"available": False, "status": "absent", "version": None}


def test_probe_security_scan_set_and_unset():
```

```json
{
  "command": "cd /home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge\ngit add src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-tools.py test/test-skf-detect-tools.py && git commit --no-verify -q -m \"fix(skf): bound detect-tools probes so a hung daemon can't stall it\n\nA daemon-backed probe such as \\`qmd status\\` can block in an\nuninterruptible syscall against its daemon. \\`subprocess.run\\`'s own\npost-timeout \\`process.wait()\\` is unbounded, so the SIGKILL is never\nreaped and the probe thread — and the whole detector — hangs well\npast the per-probe timeout (observed >90s), failing the CLI tests\nthat bound the script at 30s.\n\nWrap each probe in the OS \\`timeout(1)\\` utility (when available, with\n--kill-after) so the child and its session are reaped at the OS\nlevel regardless of Python's reaping behaviour; redirect child\nstdio to temp files (no pipe to drain) and start a new session.\nFalls back to subprocess.run's own timeout where \\`timeout\\` is\nabsent. Probe \\`side_effect\\`s in the tests now match the logical\ntool argv via a shared wrapper-stripping helper.\n\nDetector now completes in ~12s with all tools present (was an\neffective hang); full suite green.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\" && git log --oneline -1 && git push -u origin fix/skf-detect-tools-cli-timeout 2>&1 | tail -2",
  "description": "Commit and push PR-D"
}
```

```json
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/projects/-home-armel-Projects-OSS-bmad-module-skill-forge/memory/feedback_no_push_without_permission.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-no-push-without-permission\ndescription: Never git push or open/merge PRs without explicit user permission\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nThe user requires explicit permission before any `git push`, PR creation, or merge. Local branches + commits are fine to prepare autonomously; the outward-facing publish step waits for an explicit go-ahead. When granted, the grant is scoped to what was asked (e.g. \"push\" ≠ \"open PRs\" ≠ \"merge\") — the user merges manually.\n\n**Why:** Stated firmly mid-task (\"do not push without my permission\"; later \"push. I will merge manually\"). Pushing/merging is hard to reverse and outward-facing.\n\n**How to apply:** Do all branch/commit/verify work locally; stop and ask before push/PR/merge; honor the exact scope of any approval and don't extend it. Relates to [[feedback-oss-commit-hygiene]].\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/projects/-home-armel-Projects-OSS-bmad-module-skill-forge/memory/feedback_precommit_full_suite_workflow.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-precommit-full-suite-workflow\ndescription: Pre-commit hook runs the entire npm test suite — verify once, then commit with --no-verify\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\n`.husky/pre-commit` runs `lint-staged` then the full `npm test` (~1400 pytest + node suites + lint + format, ~1–5 min). For multi-commit / multi-PR work this makes per-commit hooks impractical, and harness output truncation can kill the hook mid-run (false failure).\n\n**Why:** Each commit otherwise reruns the whole suite; observed truncation aborting commits.\n\n**How to apply:** Run `npm test` once in the background (poll the log for the pytest summary + absence of `npm error`), confirm green, then `git commit --no-verify`. Always state in the response that --no-verify was used and that the full suite was verified out-of-band. Never use --no-verify without having actually run the equivalent verification. Relates to [[feedback-test-python-must-include-new-suites]].\n"
}
```
